The reasons for the adoption of this law, in our opinion, are, above all, in the fact that Serbia is a country that is extremely unevenly developed. The relationship between developed and undeveloped regions is 15:1, which by all standards and criteria is considered extremely unacceptable and presents a serious problem in the country's overall economic development. We all know, and that's what everyone here has been saying, these are the facts, that in Serbia, due to uneven economic development, parts of the country are empty, that huge centres are being created that have no capacity to develop naturally, therefore, and the whole country has no capacity and the ability to develop economically faster. We have been defined for Serbia's entry into the EU and all we are doing in this assembly is, among other things, achieving that intention. One of the assumptions of Serbia's faster entry into the EU is even regional development. So this is not only the indirect interest of Serbian citizens, it is also the interest of Serbian citizens because achieving the objectives of European integration is related to balanced economic development. The European Union attaches special importance to the equal development and co-operation of the region in Europe, and this is also confirmed by the fact that it is making huge resources stand aside for the more balanced economic development of the country. In this way, we create legal assumptions that Serbia is creating legal conditions for more balanced economic development, on the one hand, and, on the other hand, to allow European integration to approach the funds that are intended for more balanced economic development in the process of European integration. This is where the proposing's intention is questioned, his good or bad intention to abuse something of this law. I would not be particularly concerned with this, because I consider it a purely political category in all our polemics, not arguments, but a political reckoning with political inexperiences. For us, this is a law sent to the Assembly by the Government of the Republic of Serbia and we have never discussed who is in a formal sense, which the ministry is proposing. The law was passed by the Government of the Republic of Serbia, and we view it as the law of the Government of the Republic of Serbia. So this law is not about Serbia's regionalisation, this law is a law on even economic development. What we could ask as certain questions related to this law is the fact that there is a difference in the position of what statistical regions are. While Vojvodina and Belgrade give an opinion, other regions do not have an institutional framework to participate in how funds will be planned and spent in their regions. We believe that this is a matter that is still necessary in the course of this debate to resolve, to find a way to amend and define it, because one reason was that Serbia is a country that has one part of its territory defined by the Constitution, which is defined as autonomy, both political and territorial autonomy, and has more competences and rights when its citizens are concerned than citizens from other parts of Serbia. In our deep belief, the most important issue, which needs to be specified by this law, is how other statistical regions will also be able to influence through some institutional form, how they will be organised, what representative bodies will be, which will also have the opportunity to influence how the region's development plans will be made, and how funds will be spent, which is one of the key issues. Unless the issue is defined, we will not equalise the position of all citizens in Serbia with this law and again citizens in Vojvodina and citizens in Belgrade will be privileged to the citizens of other statistical regions. Indeed, it may be a matter of precision, or definition of Article 1. If this isn't the regional law, then we think Article 1 should look different. Given the need for Serbia to develop more evenly regionally, given the need for Serbia to accept all the values that are in force in the EU countries, which we are seeking, by accepting the fact that the issue of access to funds intended for equal regional development is very important for Serbia, we will support this law, but we think that some of these issues to the day of adoption of the law must be defined, in order, first of all, to remove the dilemma of what this law is and, secondly, in order to remove this unequal position of Serbian citizens living outside Vojvodina and outside Belgrade.

This means that citizens at that regional level can discuss that not every place, neither possible nor economically justified, has its own hospital. Many other problems, a nursing home, a home for old and weary faces cannot have every municipality, but within the region, you can agree where these centres will be and make it possible for these citizens to really benefit from regionalization, to be exactly what I was talking about. I have prepared to discuss what Europe of the region is, because often, in use, the problem that still does not exist is the accepted definition of the region, and can be understood as a cultural, economic, social or administrative region. Within the framework of regional structures, EU structural and cohesion policies, under the region, mainly involve administrative regions as territories that are assembling and subject to common competence for administrative reasons. It is of particular importance that this is the first administrative level below the state level. Representatives of this concept, as a result of the growing danger of unwanted centralisation of power at European Union level, suggest that citizenship sees stronger regionalisation of the EU as a counterweight for centralisation and within the EU itself, which warns the decline of EU acceptability and the thesis of "marrow" enlargement. Regional policy promoters are, above all, Germany, Belgium, Spain, Italy and Austria since its entry in 1995. Although the introduction of the Committee of the Regions has seen resistance from national governments and centralist EU countries, a trend can be seen in the European Union to gradually strengthen the regional level, although regional substructures, such as in Greece, Finland, Ireland and Poland, mentioned today, cannot be compared to powerful German provinces, for example. Since there was no effective representation of regional interests in the European Union, in 1985 a Assembly of European Regions, based in Strasbourg, which today belongs to about 300 European regions from Western and Eastern Europe, was established. Since the start of the work of the Committee of the Regions, in March 1994, the Assembly of the European Regions has lost importance, but today it has a role to connect the regions and the EU countries with other European countries. Today, the Committee of the Regions has over 300 representatives of the region and of the communal levels, who are advisoryly taking part in EU legislation, and a significant link to EU decision-making is regional bureaus in Brussels, with several hundred representations and lobbying bureaus. These are just some of the thesis that I wanted to say something about, precisely because here often, with a lot of misunderstanding and ignorance of the EU structure, talk about this problem. In addition to Europe's nations, Europe's citizens and Europe's minorities, today's Europe is more and more Europe's region, with more than 300, as I have said, the euro region. From 2000 to 2006, the EU financed transnational co-operation of border and other regions with 4,875 billion euros through the Interact programme, which clearly speaks of possible profits from Serbian and regional citizens from joining the EU. Since its introduction in 1975, the share of regional policy has been 1.2%, to rise to 7.8% in 1989, and cohesion structural funds have reached 36% of the total Union funds and account for 80% of total budget costs with the agricultural fund. The structural-political principles of EU regional policy are three basic objectives: the improvement of the lagging regions, which are those that do not reach more than 75% of the EU average, the economic and social transformation of areas with structural problems, such as deindustry, unemployment, population reduction and the like, and the adaptation and modernisation of education and employment policy. All these objectives contained in the structural-political principles of EU regional policy are also contained in the objectives proposed by this law, something extended to specificities. We, the DS, will vote in favour of this proposal for a law on regional development, and we really need to do everything possible to ensure that this regionalisation is for the benefit of citizens and that something called Serbia's feudality is prevented. Serbia's feud may seem acceptable to someone, from the point of view that it would like to become a feeder, but from the point of view of most citizens, it is not acceptable and it is not the aim of this law or the future regionalisation of Serbia. I would like to repeat once again, we want a regionalisation that will not mean disintegration of Serbia, which will mean increased participation of citizens in public affairs and will mean strengthening state integrity. That's why we're gonna back this bill in voting day.

Our proposal, when the time comes for amendments, is for local self-government, if indeed the MPs care about this decentralisation, to organise themselves, to launch initiatives and to form regions according to the only logical principles, which are economic, economic, historical, cultural, traffic, infrastructure, to make that initiative to the government and to give the government, based on this information, a decision. Is this a better solution and is it a true return of rights to local self-government to organize, thus and to citizens, or is it a democratic solution to... (President: Time. I'm done. ... One administrative act, or one draft law, suddenly appears some seven regions, as I said, politically disputed, from the point of view of the Constitution, also disputed. Forty-four are amendments, we will explain each and every one to you, and we will prove to you that the DSS has an incomparably better programme for Serbia's regionalisation, because it is our motive – the interest of citizens and the right of citizens to regionalisation and decentralisation of government.

ParlaMint-RS_2009-06-09-0.u21851	Madam President, distinguished members of the government, ladies and gentlemen, I really won't be long, but I just want to say that this is not a law on regionalisation, although DS advocates regionalisation of Serbia, this is not a law on decentralisation, although the DS advocates decentralisation of government, deeply convinced that it is a presumption of faster, more even development, for the greater participation of Serbian citizens in deciding on their own destiny. It is very unusual that someone, for everything he proposes, claims to be working on behalf of the citizens, contesting anyone else that what he is doing also works in the interests of the citizens. We can differ in how we see the best solution for citizens, but I don't think it's politically correct, except that it's not true, to challenge others' right to do something in the interests of citizens. The democratic party does not need to be given orders, since we deeply believe in the European idea, we deeply believe in all the reasons for Serbia to go in haste towards the EU, we deeply believe in Serbia's internal needs to comply with its own laws with the EU, even when it is, as some say, prematurely compared to Serbia's current status in European integration. That is why it is of particular importance for us to transform Serbia in line with the developed world, and this developed world does not prove that there is anything other than the fact that people live better there. The only interest that we are also doing is in Serbia's citizens and our true desire for Serbian citizens to live as well as citizens of Europe. This law is not a law on decentralisation. The law also did not give up the constitutional obligation and the willingness of the DS to wear out all constitutional capacity and content of Vojvodina's autonomy in the statute and jurisdiction law. This law is, in our deep confidence, something quite different, and we've talked a lot about it, and that's why we're going to support it, believing that the government will have hearing and understanding for some amendments. I am not saying that some of the amendments cannot correct the text of this law and I believe that the Government will carefully, applying the concept of the law, consider all amendments and accept all those who will contribute to making the text of the law better. Thank you.
